It seemed as though his liberated soul, having merged into the Cosmos, continued to pose that same question over and over again: "Tell me, who is mad, you or I?"

---

## Why is There Opposition to Ananda Marga?

From its very inception Ananda Marga has had to face opposition, and right from those early years, the organization has had to struggle against hostile forces. Baba often used to say that only progressive thoughts and ideas meet with resistance. Traditional, conservative, even insular thinking, never meets with any opposition. In fact conflict is a sure sign of progress. Without struggle, success is impossible. Indeed there is a close link between struggle and the effort to reach one's goal. Obstacles and hindrances are but indicators of progress.

A story related by Acarya Bhaskaranandaji is appropriate in this context. It is about a small town in which the traders and merchants had all combined together to loot and exploit the consumers. One day a new trader came to that town and began to sell all his goods at a reasonable price. Gradually his business began to thrive and soon there was a continuous stream of customers in his shop. At the same time, the other shopkeepers began to lose their customers and as their business declined they were left with nothing to do. Following the example of the new trader, some young merchants also began to sell their goods at fair prices. Gradually their business too improved. By then the old greedy merchants were burning with envy. They got together and conspired to do away with the handful of honest traders. But they were exposed and thwarted from carrying out their evil designs.

Ananda Marga faces that same situation in which the honest trader found himself when he arrived in the town and began selling his goods at a fair price. Under Baba's direction, some industrious and diligent people eager to serve society took up the challenge of creating a sound structure for a just and ideal human society. As a result, the traditionalists and the affluent felt seriously threatened. They determined to destroy this movement with its new ideology, and towards that end countless weapons and arms began to be employed.

In the early years of the Marga, that is in the fifties and sixties of the last century, the opposition to it came from those Brahmins who encouraged traditional ideas of caste divisions, and from members of the Arya Samaj. Ananda Marga came down heavily

on all traditional sacrificial ceremonial rites and religious dogmas. It also opposed the wearing of the religious thread and the sporting of the lock of unshaved hair, for it considered them as mere signs of caste distinctions. The conservatives were agitated and enraged by this attack on traditional customs.

Impressed and inspired by the Marga's call for the renewal of moral and ethical values in society, many government and semi-government officers were drawn to it. These officers became instrumental in checking the bribery and corruption prevalent in government offices. This was an intolerable situation for the government and its greedy and corrupt officers. So by way of revenge, the government sought to malign the Marga. It made full use of false and malicious propaganda and leveled outrageous charges in order to vilify the organization and its members. Gradually the true facts emerged and the Marga was vindicated. But the stains of defamation and vilification could not all be washed away, and to this day the Marga suffers on account of the same.

Shri Prabhat Ranjan Sarkar's splendid and balanced social treatise based on "*Sama-Samaja Tattva*" (Principle of Social Equality), calls for the creation of a just and healthy society, one controlled by *sadvipras*. However, in this thesis, there is no scope for any exploitation by the capitalists or the communists. In fact there is no role even for the so-called socialists, for their plans and programs lack flexibility and are bounded by factors of time and space. PROUT (Progressive Utilisation Theory), propounded and established by Shri Sarkar, shook the very foundation of the then stronghold of communism, Russia. It felt threatened, and as a result it initiated intense intrigue both at the political and diplomatic levels to uproot the Marga, and thereby crush the organization.

However this *hari-katha* relates to the days when opposition to the Marga was still in its infancy. In those days it was the Arya Samajists and the caste-driven traditionalists who were the main opponents of the Marga. The attitude of the Arya Samaj became intensely hostile to the Marga and they began to disrupt and interfere in all its activities. Ananda Marga did not believe in continuing with traditional rites and rituals, including the sacred thread ceremony, and did not support these. The conservatives construed this as an attack on the Hindu Dharma, on Hindu traditions and culture. According to Ananda Marga, the sacred thread and the lock of unshaved hair were signs and symbols of those who encouraged and fostered casteism, a concept which no longer had any validity today. Casteism brings about division in society, whereas human society is one and indivisible. The characteristics of *Vaeshya*, *Vipra*, *Kshatriya* and *Shudra* are no doubt present in all mankind, but they help to determine one's innate temperament and character and not one's caste. The concept that one's caste is indelibly stamped on account of one's birth is totally illogical.

The Arya Samajists refused to accept these ideas and instead labeled the Marga as an anti-Hindu organization, and tried to defame it. At times they would call it a communist organization, at other times they would accuse it of having pro-Islamic leanings. The situation became volatile, conflicts and clashes followed, and there were frequent confrontations between the Ananda Margiis and the Arya Samajists. In those days there were just a handful of Margiis, while the Arya Samajists enjoyed a pre-eminent status in society, on account of which they could sway public opinion. False propaganda was their main tactic, and they tried every possible means to create discord and antagonism against the Marga. Whenever someone rented out his property to the Marga, to run its school or its ashram, the Arya Samajists would forthwith poison his ears and antagonize the party. Thus they would try very hard to get the Margiis evicted from their rented premises.

The Arya Samajists would make sure to attend every religious assembly organized by the Marga and would draw the Margiis into a doctrinal debate. If a Dharma Mahacakra was being held they would insist on gaining unauthorized entry into the venue. No doubt they would invariably suffer a reverse, and their intentions could never be practically realized. The following incident is an example of one such unsuccessful attempt.

This incident took place in the year 1955, in a village called Pathargam, close to Bhagalpur in Bihar. A Dharma Mahacakra was being organized in that village and the Arya Samajists somehow got wind of this. They lost no time in declaring that they would not allow the *mahacakra* to take place, and threatened to disrupt the function if it was held. Pathargam was an Arya Samaj stronghold, where they wielded much influence and authority. The local Margiis were very worried on account of this hostile attitude of the Arya Samajists. It was only because of their implicit faith in Baba that they decided to stick to their plans, and did not postpone the program. Nonetheless, their minds were filled with the nagging fear that anti-social elements would create all sorts of trouble for them.

As per the announcement, Baba arrived on schedule and stayed in the house of a Margii by the name of Ramprasad. The Dharma Mahacakra was to be held in an enclosed compound situated on the

main road. On the day of the mahacakra, the opposition forces clearly announced their intention of seeking entry into the premise. They further threatened to force their way into the enclosure in case an objection was raised against their admission. The Margiis patiently tried to explain to them that as per the rules of the organization, an uninitiated person could not participate in a Dharma Mahacakra function. In other words they could take *diiksha* and get admitted.

The entreaties fell on deaf ears, for the opponents stood stubbornly by their demands. A crowd had gathered outside the gate and none amongst them was willing to budge or leave. A few sturdy and robust volunteers of the Marga were posted at the gate. But they were only five to seven, whereas the crowd was five to seven hundred strong! If they were to turn violent or merely start to push and jostle, it would have been impossible to halt them. The organizers were concerned about the safety of the ladies seated inside, but the most important question was of assuring Baba's security. Some of the miscreants threw stones into the enclosure and a young *sadhika* just missed being hurt. Very soon the situation became alarmingly dangerous.

Just then Baba sent a message from within which required that all the Margiis who were posted at the gate to halt the crowd be called inside. This order was not readily acceptable to the volunteers. After all why should they leave their post? If they did so, it would imply that they were openly inviting the crowd to enter and thereby disrupt the proceedings. This was in fact their clear intention. On the other hand Baba's orders could no longer be evaded. Finally, with full faith and trust in Him, they surrendered all their fears and responsibilities, the very gravity of the situation itself, at His Lotus Feet, and went inside.

No sooner had they entered inside, their minds now at peace, than sparks were seen emitting from an electricity pole just outside the gate. Even as the sparks exploded and began to spread quickly, panic broke out in the crowd. The sparks flew from one pole to another and suddenly the power went off. The whole area was plunged in darkness and there was a stampede in the crowd. People began to run helter-skelter. The situation was now reversed; the protesters who had come to create a disturbance were themselves thrown into a state of chaos, and very soon the crowd had dispersed. As they watched the situation the Margii organizers were dumbstruck by Baba's wondrous miracle. This was not all, for when the electric current went off, there was total darkness inside the enclosure too. But miraculously, the bulb on the stage where Baba was seated did not go off. It continued to glow, reflecting as it did the 'flame of faith' in the minds of all the *sadhakas*. From then onwards, the Dharma Mahacakra went off smoothly.

The next day too, there was a program organized for Baba. At the time of His field walk, He was scheduled to visit the Ananda Marga ashram and grace it with His holy presence. The ashram was close by, situated in a place called Balabhadrapur. As per the program, Baba arrived at the ashram, hoisted the flag and blessed everyone. However, instead of proceeding onwards, He continued to remain seated in the ashram. Generally Baba's program would be so crowded and busy that there would be no time for relaxation. Hence it was all the more surprising that He should spare time to sit there in a leisurely and relaxed manner.

Acarya Kuldipji was a very senior and experienced officer. He easily saw through this most unlikely and unusual situation. Immediately he got into his jeep, and along with some of his colleagues set off to survey the road that lay ahead, to inspect whether it was safe to travel by. After having driven just a short distance from the ashram, they saw that some hostile elements had broken down the embankments of a pond, as a result of which the road had been heavily flooded. Simultaneously, they had also cut off the road, making it impossible to traverse it by jeep either.

Immediately Kuldipji got some people together and within three quarters of an hour was able to get the road repaired, enough so that a car could go across. Thereafter he returned to the ashram. Just as he reached, he saw that Baba was already seated in the car and was ready to return. By then Kuldipji was able to understand full well the secret behind Baba's apparent relaxation. Baba's car was able to cross the road smoothly and He was able to reach the quarters where He had been put up without any trouble along the way. Immediately thereafter, the succeeding program began as per schedule. This time too the Arya Samajists were unsuccessful in carrying out their disruptive activities.

After the Dharma Mahacakra, as Baba was taking leave of everyone, some senior Margiis, overcome by a sense of guilt, entered Baba's room and sought His forgiveness for the lapses in the organization of the program. Acarya Ramapraveshji said, "Baba! It is certainly because of our lack of devotion that we encountered so many obstacles and our arrangements were disrupted."

To this Baba replied, "Not at all Ramapravesh! Obstacles, obstructions, resistance and hostility are all an indication of progress. They are your veritable symbols of progress, both in your spiritual and organizational endeavours. Never allow yourself to be shaken by

difficulties and afflictions. You yourself will experience that once the trouble passes away, your minds will be far more elevated and controlled. Yes, difficulties are bound to come, but they never last for ever. Ignoring all the thorns, pebbles and stones that may come in your way, you must continue to move ahead. Victory is certainly yours. No demonic spirit, evil or anti-social force can ever defeat you."

In this context Baba's New Year's *vani* dated January 1, 1958, is particularly relevant:

> Don't compare life to a pool of muddy, stagnant water. Life resembles an everflowing spring. Pushing aside the stones of obstruction and difficulties, marching on with vigorous speed is its nature, its Dharma. Hence it is clear that he who wants to keep away from obstacles has lost the Dharma of life – verily, he is dead. The graveyard, not society should be his abode.

One of the Margiis mentioned to Baba that in the stone-throwing incident on Dharma Mahacakra day, a young *sadhika* narrowly missed being hurt. Baba replied, "This is not something to worry about. The forces that support Dharma will always protect you. Let it also be known that you do not need to harbour any ill-feelings towards your opponents. Do you know that the very boy who threw that stone will one day become a staunch member of the Marga?"

Indeed that was exactly what happened. The boy took diiksha, became an LFT and served as a responsible LFT teacher in the school at Pathargam. Each one of Baba's liilas is more amazing than the other, indeed they are beyond, rather far beyond the comprehension of the human mind!

---

> *Dharma eva hato hanti, dharmo rakshati rakshitah.*
>
> ("Dharma, when assailed, destroys; protected, it protects.")

---

## Unconditional Grace Showered upon the Prime Minister

Several individuals and organizations, swayed by vested interests, vociferously and enthusiastically carried out the malicious propaganda that Ananda Marga was bent on assassinating leading Indian politicians. Word was spread around that the Marga workers carried a 'hit list' of targeted politicians, and were fully prepared to carry out their murderous attacks. It thus came about that the Ananda Marga was unjustly and needlessly blamed for the death of the Bihar minister, Shrii Lalit Narayan Mishra. Indira Gandhi, who died at the hands of the Khalistanis, used to live in constant fear of a possible attack from an Ananda Margii.

The same fear was driven into the mind of erstwhile Morarji Desai too. He was made to believe that Ananda Margiis posed a real threat to his life. As a result of this false publicity and negative propaganda, the Marga was thoroughly disgraced; its reputation was in shambles. But the facts of the situation were quite contrary to what was publicly projected.

The perpetrators of these lies succeeded in getting Baba arrested and jailed. (Later, on August 2, 1978, He was honourably acquitted by the judiciary.) While Baba was in jail, the secret service branch of the government spread the word that some revolutionary members of the Marga were conspiring to use forceful means to extricate Him from jail. By that time, eight incidents of self-immolation had already taken place. These incidents were a demonstration of protest against Baba's detention and the subsequent atrocities perpetrated on Him. Helpless in securing the release of their most revered Guru, these acts were borne of inner conviction and could not be likened to the vengeful killings of seasoned criminals. These incidents of supreme sacrifice took place both in India and abroad. As a consequence, false allegations were leveled against the Marga. Word was spread that efforts were being made by some passionate and revolutionary members of the Marga to forcefully obtain Baba's release, without a judicial adjudication.

Around that time, the then Prime Minister, Morarjee Desai happened to be attending the Commonwealth Conference in Australia. The conference was being held in the Hilton Hotel, in Canberra. There was a bomb blast close to the hotel where the conference was being held. The Australian government, acting on the advice of the Indian government, rounded up some local Margiis and took them into custody. A criminal lawsuit was filed against them. In the end, all the accused were declared to be innocent. In fact the Australian government, by way of making amends for the false charges and the prolonged detention that the accused had to undergo, compensated them handsomely. It is now well known that the accusations of conspiring to assassinate Morarjee Desai were baseless and entirely unfounded.

While all the aspersions and malicious allegations against the Marga were given utmost publicity, the actual facts of the situation, when they emerged, were not publicized at all. In fact only a handful of people were in the know of the raw facts. Fewer still must have any knowledge about the incident that is being related here. As the facts of this incident emerge, it is abundantly clear that it was Baba who saved Morarjee Desai from the throes of death and gave him a new life.

This incident took place on February 12, 1973. Baba was imprisoned in Bankipur Jail, in Patna. He was being tried for a charge of murder that had been falsely leveled against Him. While He was in jail, there was a conspiracy to eliminate Him. In the garb of medicine, poison was administered to Him. Baba had written to the government demanding a judicial inquiry into the poisoning incident. However, thanks to Indiraji's 'favourable' leanings towards Baba, no inquiry was held. Finally, by way of registering His protest, Baba announced that He would go on an indefinite fast until such time as an inquiry was initiated. In spite of this the government was not moved to act; in fact it stubbornly chose to turn a deaf ear to Baba's legitimate demand. Baba always stood by His principles—in this He was very strict indeed. He began His fast and no one could persuade Him to change His mind. Atal Bihari Vajpayee, the present Prime Minister of India, personally met with Baba and tried to dissuade Him, but Baba refused to yield. Finally after much pleading from the Margiis, Baba agreed to take buttermilk, only once a day. This too, on the condition, that the yogurt would be brought from His own home. He absolutely refused to accept anything from the government. An appeal was filed in the High Court pleading that under special circumstances, the Honourable Court should grant this request. The Court gave its consent but stipulated that only one particular person (Baba's Personal Assistant, Ramanandaji alone) would bring the yogurt from Baba's house and deliver it to His cell. This procedure was strictly followed and it continued for five years and five months.

One day as per the regular routine, Ramanandaji, after having served the buttermilk to Baba, was returning by cycle rickshaw to Baba's quarters. Accompanying him in the rickshaw was another Margii, Mahendraji, who was also a resident of Patna. Suddenly, a car that seemed to be recklessly driven rammed into their rickshaw from behind and sped away. On account of the severe impact, Mahendraji and the rickshaw driver were flung onto the road and Ramanandaji, along with the overturned rickshaw, fell into a ditch. Due to the tremendous shock, Ramanandaji became unconscious on the spot.

Mahendraji and the rickshaw driver were only slightly grazed, and the two of them managed to bring Ramanandaji, in this unconscious state, to Baba's quarters at Pataliputra Colony. There was a great stir in the quarters and they immediately called for a doctor. Medical treatment continued for a long while and it was nearly midnight before Ramanandaji showed some signs of consciousness. As soon as he regained consciousness, Ramanandaji was told the details of the accident. He was in a bad state as the injuries were severe and the pain unbearable. However, he was least concerned about his own condition. Instead he was now worried about how the curds would be delivered to Baba on the following day. The attitude of the jail authorities was most uncooperative. Under the circumstances, to expect them to agree to allow another person to bring the yogurt for Baba was like asking for the impossible. There was no scope either for another judicial appeal.

Ramanandaji was frantically worried about Baba's well-being. He was in no condition to move around, in fact he was barely able to sit up. However, the next day, as the hour approached for him to take the yogurt, Ramanandaji found to his utter amazement that his pain had suddenly vanished. At the same time, he experienced a sudden surge of strength and energy in his body. So it was, that as per the regulation, Ramanandaji himself took the yogurt to the jail at the appointed time.

It so happened that at the very moment when Ramanandaji met with the accident, some Margiis who had come for Baba's darshan were with Him in His cell. Dr. Dharamdas Kalwar, who was the jail

doctor, was also present there, and Baba was talking to them. Suddenly He broke off the conversation. Expressing a sense of relief as though a grave disaster had been averted, He exclaimed, "Saved! Saved!"

The visiting Margiis were taken aback by this sudden interjection and change in Baba's mood. For a while they kept staring at Him intently. One of them mustered up courage and asked, "What happened, Baba?"

Baba replied, "You know, at this very moment, two very important men of our country were about to meet with instantaneous death, but due to divine grace, both of them have had a narrow escape." Having said this, Baba heaved a sigh of relief and asked the doctor who was seated nearby to immediately bring Him a tulsi leaf. The doctor rushed out and returned with the tulsi leaf. Baba took the leaf in His hand and with eyes shut, silently recited a mantra. He then returned the leaf to Dr. Kalwar and instructed him to keep it safely with him, for He would call for it when needed.

The next day when Ramanandaji arrived with the yogurt and entered the cell, Baba greeted him with great affection, His heart overflowing with deep fatherly love and concern. He said to Ramanandaji, "Come, come Ramananda! How are you now? Yesterday you were to meet with certain death. And do you know that by your grace alone the Prime Minister of the country, Morarjee Desai too was saved!" The news that Morarjee Desai had been involved in a flight accident and had a narrow escape had been announced on the radio the previous night. The next day's papers too carried bold headlines about the Prime Minister's miraculous escape. The fact of the situation was that Morarjee Desai was travelling by plane and the aircraft was approaching Jorhat in the North Eastern state of Assam. Suddenly, due to some engine trouble, the pilot was forced to make a crash landing. The pilot and the flight attendant were killed on the spot but due to providential grace, Morarjee Desai had a narrow escape.

Baba continued to explain in greater detail. He told Ramanandaji that at the very moment when his rickshaw was hit by the recklessly speeding car, the plane in which the Prime Minister was travelling had crashed too. Instantaneous death, for both of them, was unavoidable. Baba then said that it was His duty to save Ramanandaji. But had He saved Ramanandaji, and not saved the Prime Minister of the country, then He would have been accused of partiality. "That is why," Baba went on to say, "the Prime Minister was saved by your grace."

Ramanandaji was at a loss for words. He was overcome with emotion, even as he realized Baba's unconditional grace. Baba then turned to Dr. Dharamdas Kalwar, who was seated beside Him, and asked him to bring the *tulsi* leaf He had given him the day before. Baba then told Ramanandaji to accept the sanctified leaf by way of *prasad*.

As long as Ramanandaji was in the jail with Baba and up to the time that he returned back to the quarters, he felt well and normal. The moment he reached the quarters, the unbearable pain returned. This pattern continued for a full week. When the time approached for him to take the yogurt for Baba, Ramanandaji would feel well and strong. The moment he would return after having fulfilled his duty, he would once more experience the severe pain.

From the above story it is clear and worth noting that "no one can harm the one whom the Lord protects." Simultaneously, the reader can draw his own conclusions as to who graced Morarjee Desai with the gift of a new life!

> *ruciinám vaecitryád kutilanánápathajushám,*
> *nrnám eko gamyas tvam asi payasám arnava iva.*
>
> ("For human beings, who pursue crooked and various paths, because of the variety of their inclinations, You alone are the destination, like the ocean for the waters.")

## What Should We Ask from the Lord?

The Supreme Father is the Lord of the universe, the controller of every action in this world. Life and Death are in His hands. By His mere wish, life can be terminated or death transformed into a new lease of life. So it is very easy for Him to grant whatever we may ask from Him. All He needs to say is "*tathástu*" ("Let it be thus") and in a trice, as though with the mere snap of His fingers, one's request is fulfilled. But is it proper for us to ask anything from Him? Implied in our request is a covert accusation against our beloved Father. Surely we have no right to do so. It is because He has not given us the object of our desires, or the condition in life we may long for, that we entreat Him to grant us these! So are we presuming that our Father is unaware of our wants and needs? Are we not too ready to point a finger at His capability to dispense divine justice? Besides, if we must ask for something, why should we ask for some cheap worldly objects? Why not ask for devotion or for the capacity to surrender at His feet? Indeed, why do we not ask for Him alone?

In this context the story of the boatman in *Ramacharitamanasa* is both relevant and instructive. When Ram was exiled into the forests, Janaki and Lakshman also accompanied him. In order to cross the Ganges, they had to take the help of a boatman. By the very touch of Ram's holy feet the boatman's wooden boat was transformed into gold. The boatman's wife was amazed to see the power and the glory emanating from the Lord's holy feet. She quickly ran back and forth and brought out all the wooden items from her house. One by one she placed them at the Lord's feet and by their touch the objects turned to gold. When she was totally exhausted and about to collapse, the boatman said to her: "Foolish woman! Instead of exerting yourself as you have been doing, carrying these heavy loads from there to here, would it not be better if you took Lord Ram himself to our home?" The boatman's wife came to her senses and realized her mistake. We human beings repeatedly commit the same mistake. Foolishly we run after material objects and in the process we forget the One who is the Lord and Master of all this wealth.

There is a sequel to this story. The boatman and his wife take Ram, Lakshman and Sita to their home. Ram is very pleased with them and presents them with four fruits, *dharma*, *artha*, *kama* and *moksha*. Thereupon the boatman's wife turns to Lakshman and says, "Will you not give us anything?" Lakshman replies that they had already received everything from his respected brother, so what more could they want?

Just then, Sita nudged the boatman's wife and said, "No! Don't accept his excuses. You must certainly take something from him also." After much persuasion, Lakshman said that he had only one thing which he could possibly give to them. And so he offered them the gift of devotion.

Thereupon Sita said, "You know, this is the best of all the gifts. For what will you do with the gifts of *dharma*, *artha*, *kama* and *moksha* if you do not have devotion?"

Bound by *samskaras*, we humans are wrapped up in worldliness and that is how we trip and fall. Forgetting the butter we run after the butter-milk. This false and deceitful daughter of the wild, Maya, beguiles us with these very diluted pleasures. Intoxicated, we begin to dance to her tune and continue to do so all through our life. The very essence and core of our existence lies in *bhakti*, devotion. But in our absorption with worldly life and living we forget this—and that is where we fail.

Why should we ask for something trivial and cheap from the Lord? If we must ask for something let us only ask for that wonderful, precious gift of *bhakti*. But we are not able to bring ourselves to do so. During Baba's lifetime, so many persons of good *samskaras* came in contact with Him. They had realized His divinity, had recognized His excellence and greatness. But when an opportunity came their way, they ended up by asking for that same "butter-milk." They allowed themselves to forget that fundamental and most significant factor, *bhakti*.

In the above context, this particular incident is worth relating. Lakshminayak was a young Margii from the Arariya Zilla of Bihar. As he came in contact with Baba, he was much impressed by His qualities of omnipotence and omniscience. Soon he became a devoted and dedicated *sadhaka*. Once he too got an opportunity to ask something from Baba. But what did he ask of Him? It was on a Saturday when he said to Baba, "Baba! My father has been suffering from an acute pain in the stomach. It causes him much distress. But by Your grace and mercy he could be relieved of his suffering." No doubt he was a little

afraid and diffident to ask, but he could not help making the request. His father had been suffering from the stomach ailment for a very long time. At times the attack would be so severe that he would writhe with pain and become unconscious. For the last thirty years he had been complaining of this unbearable pain. The entire family had been distressed by his suffering. So it was that although the sadhaka knew that it was not proper for him to ask, he could not resist pleading for His compassionate grace.

That very day, in the course of His discourse, Baba had said that the only way to get release from the bondage of samskaras is by undergoing the consequences of their reactions. There was no other "short cut" available for human beings. Earlier too, the Margii gentleman had spoken to Baba about his father's illness. But Baba had remained silent. Once again the *sadhaka* brought up the same topic, and this time Baba said to him, "If you are able to make him give up smoking tobacco then his stomach problem will go away by itself." The father was addicted to smoking, a fact which the *sadhaka* had never revealed to Baba. Baba once again questioned him: "You will make him give up smoking, won't you?"

"Yes Baba!" The *sadhaka* replied, thumping his chest by way of assurance.

Immediately thereafter, the sadhaka wrote a letter to his father entreating him to give up smoking. He further explained that by doing so the stomach pain would be gone forever. His father promptly replied to say that even before receiving the letter he had already given up smoking. In the letter he related how a *sannyasii* had appeared to him in a dream and had stressed upon him the need to give up smoking and chewing tobacco. This alone would cure him of his stomach ailment. The father further wrote to say that he had followed these instructions and for the last two days he had felt no pain at all.

Having read the letter, the *sadhaka's* joy knew no bounds. Not only had Baba prescribed a remedy, He had also, through the dream, enabled his father to get rid of his wretched and deadly habit. He was unable to contain himself and was eager to share his joy. So he quickly went and met his Acarya, Pashupatiji, and showed him the letter. Acaryaji too was very pleased. In his excitement, he caught a train from Bhagalpur and went straight home. There he repeatedly questioned his father about the identity of the sannyasii. What did he say? How did he express himself? All the father could say was that he looked like any other *sadhu*, and there was nothing unusual about him. The father and the rest of his family had not taken *diiksha*, so there was no point in asking whether the *sannyasii* bore a resemblance to any one of the *avadhuta* Dadas. Finally he gave up asking, but certainly a huge load had been lifted from his head.

The next time the *sadhaka* visited Jamalpur, Baba Himself asked him in all seriousness, "How is father?" The *sadhaka* was taken by surprise. Previously it was he who would anxiously relate to Baba about his father's condition. This time he felt it strange that Baba Himself should have inquired of him. With a deep sense of relief he informed Baba that his father had been cured of his stomach ailment. Moreover he had totally given up his habit of smoking and chewing tobacco. On hearing this, Baba smiled, but there was something mysterious about His smile.

When the *sadhaka* visited his family again, he was in for a rude shock. He learnt that his father's ailment had recurred. On probing further, he was told that for a long time his father had been secretly smoking again. The family came to know of it much later, but could anything remain hidden from Baba?

Once more the *sadhaka* was visiting Jamalpur, but this time he could not bring himself to reveal the facts to Baba. But Baba wanted to get the truth out of him and so He once again asked the *sadhaka* about his father's health. Finally he had to come out with the actual facts. Thereupon Baba said that neither by grace nor by miracles can *samskaras* be erased. Penance too does not help. Even after doing penance for millions of years, one would still have to undergo the consequences of their reactions. The good and the bad consequences—both have to be endured.

> *nábhuktaḿ kshiiyate karma kalpakotishatair api*
> *avashyam eva bhoktavyaḿ krtakarmashubhāshubham*

"Even if it takes hundreds of crores of eons, whether good or bad, actions whose results haven't been experienced will certainly be experienced by the one who did them."

Inspite of knowing this truth, we human beings cannot rise above our frailties. From time to time, we continue to entreat the Lord for His grace and mercy.

In this context there is one more story related to Baba that comes to mind. It is about a woman who lived in the vicinity of Jamalpur. She had lost her young son in a fatal accident and was unable to bear this terrible blow. She was very fond of her only son and without him life

seemed empty and meaningless. In her distress she would often sob bitterly and remained restless and disturbed. After having come in contact with the Marga she had heard that Baba could make the impossible possible. Her intense attachment to her son led her to hope that perhaps Baba could once more bring him back to life. But two years had already passed since the death of her son. How could one bring him back to life now? Besides, Baba never performed any miracles that transgressed the universal laws of Prakrti.

Although this desire of hers gradually faded away, nevertheless a slim ray of hope lingered in her mind. She also recalled having heard that Baba occasionally gave glimpses of one's past and future lives. At times He even enabled one to get in touch with the departed souls. This had once taken place in Jamalpur. On that occasion Baba had enabled a distressed widow to have a vision of her late husband. Besides this, Baba, through the medium of Dasharath Dada, used to give vivid glimpses of some of the incidents of one's past and future lives. Such stories generally spread like wild fire, and the consequences thereof can be either good or bad. They can have a beneficial effect if a *sadhaka*, inspired by such stories, experiences a surge of devotion towards his Sadguru. However, things can go very wrong for a *sadhaka* who may crave to fulfill his own selfish desires. This is exactly what happened in the case of the above-mentioned woman. Having known that Baba used Dasharathji as His medium, she was awaiting an opportunity to attend Baba's *darshana* when Dasharathji too was present.

There was another lady by the name of Kamini who also lived in Jamalpur. She used to run a small eatery where she served pure *sattvika* food. Margii brothers and sisters visiting Jamalpur for Baba's *darshana* would often take their meals at this restaurant. This sister Kamini was instrumental in reviving the hopes of the grief-stricken mother. She fanned the fires of the burning desire that the woman had continued to harbour. Kamini would advise the mother to persist in expressing her inner desire to Baba. She assured her that a day would come when the compassionate Lord would yield to her request.

The moment she was waiting for finally came her way and she was able to fulfill her long cherished desire. Baba had come to the Jamalpur Jagriti and Dasharath Dada happened to accompany Him that day. The mother seized the opportunity and once again put forth her request. This time Baba thought it absolutely necessary to put an end to this woman's wrong and improper requests. He immediately ordered the omniscient Dasharathji to peep into the new life of her deceased son.

As per Baba's orders and by His grace, Dasharathji began to visualize everything through his spiritual vision.

Dasharathji said, "Baba! His soul has now entered into a new body. He has been born into a well-placed, prosperous and noble family hailing from Guwahati."

Immediately Baba checked him and said, "Do not reveal the name and address of his new family or else this distressed woman may end up going there." Dasharathji was alerted and was cautious not to reveal any more. Instructing him further, Baba said, "Now you enter into the mind of this infant child." Dasharathji did as directed. Baba continued, "The child will not be able to speak anything. But you delve deep into his consciousness and ask his mind whether it felt any anxiety or concern about the mother of his previous life." Dasharathji did likewise and replied that the mind felt no such anxiety. Continuing to direct Dasharathji, Baba said, "go into his consciousness and see what his mental disposition would be if he was given the chance to leave his present body and return to that of his previous birth. Would he like to return?"

Penetrating into the consciousness of the child, Dasharathji told Baba, "His mind expresses great delight in its present environment. The new parents have showered him with much love and affection. He is very happy and content in his new circumstances and has no desire at all to be moved to another environment." In reply Baba asked Dasharathji to peep into the future of the child. Steering his mind beyond the realm of this physical world, Dasharathji could see that the child's future was bright and happy. As Dasharathji continued to reveal all this, the distraught mother felt consoled and her mind experienced a sense of peace. After this, she finally gave up her longing and craving for the son who had been torn away from her.

After this demonstration Baba explained, "Human relationships with one's near and dear ones last for a certain period of time. Family life can be compared to a railway journey. At every station new passengers board the train and some of the old ones get down. In the course of the journey one gets to befriend the new fellow passengers and it is natural to develop a certain warmth and affection for them. But do we shed tears when they have to get down at their respective destinations? Do we continue to think about them? No, not at all. This applies also to all family relationships. This is how you should look at it. As long as we are together, one should love one another and should

sincerely fulfill all one's duties towards the loved ones. But once the tie is broken one should not give way to grief and despair. All relationships are temporary. Only one relationship is ever lasting and that is the relationship with Parama Purusha."

Through the medium of this simple demonstration Baba has explained the entire philosophy of life and living. In His simple, clear and unambiguous language He has made us understand the true meaning of life.

> *Asitagirisamaḿ syát kajjalaḿ sindhupátre,*
> *Surataruvarashákhá lekhanii patram úrvii;*
> *Likhati yadi grhiitvá Sháradá sarvakálam,*
> *Tad api tava gunánám Iisha páram na yáti.*
>
> ("Were the ink equal to the mythic Dark Mountain, with the ocean as its fountain-well; were the pen a branch from the Tree of the Gods, with the earth as the writing surface; were the Goddess of Wisdom to take these and write for all time; even then, O Shiva, it would not be possible to approach the end of your virtues.")

---

## God's Devotees All Belong to Him

There was a time in the annals of Indian history when social reformers took up the challenging task of ridding the society of caste differences. Men of the status of Kabir, a highly evolved *sadhaka*, great poet and noble social reformer, were destined to be born in India. In those days all the social reformers raised this single slogan: "Question not one's caste or creed; God's devotees all belong to Him." There was a sense of elation as a new consciousness dawned upon the people.

But this period was short-lived. People with vested interests, who fostered and encouraged the sentiments of caste differences, became the so-called leaders of society. They once again succeeded in driving into the mass consciousness the age old feelings of hereditary caste distinctions. As a result the Brahmin, Kshatriya and Vaeshya classes came to consider themselves as superior to the Shudras. The latter consequently developed an inferiority complex. On the other hand, some political leaders, motivated by selfish interests, established their "vote banks" by playing on the feelings of caste and religious differences. From time to time, in order to safeguard their "vote banks" and increase their numbers, these politicians resurrect the ghost of caste and religious sentiments. To achieve their goals, they manipulate and incite violent riots leading to murder and arson. They have indeed plumbed the depths of human degradation.

We humans are the children of one God. Parama Purusha, the Supreme Father, is one for all of us and Parama Prakrti is our Divine Mother. So why should there be any discrimination amongst brethren? These rigid distinctions of caste and creed have been carefully created by men to serve their own interests. These sentiments help them to perpetuate their rule over the masses. Their aim is purely a selfish one, to gain power and establish their supremacy. From its very inception, Baba's mission took up the challenge of uprooting this concept of caste distinction from the social consciousness. It raised the slogan: "*Ek chula, ek chowka, ek manava samaja*" ("One kitchen, one home, one human society").

Besides this, all those who continued to adorn themselves with the signs and emblems of caste distinctions were declared unfit for initiation. So it was that those who continued to wear the sacred thread, or wore the unshaven lock of hair on the head, were refused *diiksha*, initiation. In this aspect Baba stands apart from His contemporaries. His role is unique, for He initiated a new era in the history of human society. For this He often had to face grave opposition and had to put up with slander and calumny. But He never compromised on His principles. In fact, this uncompromising strictness in upholding His principles was one of the most illuminating aspects of His personality.

Baba fiercely opposed all sentiments of caste distinction. He would be enraged if a sadhaka or a non-sadhaka was instrumental in encouraging or reviving this sentiment. One can recall many such incidents that took place in His lifetime. They are now well known. This one however is yet an untold story. The incident took place in Jamalpur.

One day, as per His daily routine Baba left His home at the opportune time and was proceeding towards the ashram. Accompanying Him was Dasharath Dada, "the one who possessed the enchanted telescope."

On the way they came across a big crowd that had gathered in front of a house. The atmosphere was grave and solemn. Feigning curiosity, Baba turned to Dasharath Dada and said, "Dasharath! Go and see what has happened – why is there such a crowd?" After making inquiries, Dasharathji conveyed to Baba that this was the house of a supposedly high-class Brahmin family. The head of the family had just expired and the entire family was overcome by grief and sadness. Relatives and friends had all gathered in mourning. The body of the deceased was being prepared for its final journey.

Baba heard all this as though He was unaware of what had taken place. As He continued to walk ahead, He turned to Dasharathji and said, "Dasharath! Look carefully and see what is the future path of progress for this disembodied mind."

Dasharath Dada had already been graced with the gift of omniscience. As he walked alongside Baba, he utilized his power and replied, "Baba! It has regressed. In the next birth it will get the body of a dog." Thereupon Baba went on to expose the real character of the deceased person. He said that although this patriarch was a prominent member of the Brahmin community, he had acquired no virtue, nor practiced any piety in his life. In fact he had fallen prey to the mental disease of excessive greed and self-gratification. Goaded by these propensities, all his actions had been sinful and wicked. He never reflected or pondered over his misdeeds. He remained lost to the world, intoxicated by his arrogance and pride derived from his Brahmin lineage. He was obsessed with the vanity of his Brahmin caste, and had developed an exaggerated sense of his superiority.

Baba went on to say, "Now when he will be reborn as a dog, what will be his state? That false sense of pride that he had in his 'lofty status', ultimately where did it get him? In spite of being a Brahmin, in his next life he will be born as a dog. What did he stand to gain? All through his life he treated the lower castes with scorn. He would disdainfully tell them to keep away from him. He was consumed with hatred and mistakenly nurtured a false sense of vanity of caste superiority. In spite of his Brahmin status, in his next life he will be a dog. All the false arrogance and conceit of Brahminhood will now be demolished. In spite of being born a Brahmin he was unable to imbibe the true spirit of Brahminhood. Just imagine his state in his next life when he takes on the body of a dog. If perchance that same dog were to stray into a gathering of Brahmins, will they not treat him as an untouchable and drive him away? With that the entire arrogant concept of 'untouchability' will be rendered meaningless. Those very people whom he hitherto considered to be untouchables will also desist from having any contact with him. Consumed as he was by his caste mentality, what did this Brahmin gain in his life? In fact he lost everything.

"Human beings should gladly accept and acknowledge the ideal of unity and equality of all men. One should draw perennial inspiration from the thought that the Supreme Father is the Supreme Spirit. With this in mind one should go ahead performing good and virtuous deeds. Herein lies man's humanity. This is the real Dharma of mankind. This truly is Sanatana Dharma. This alone is Bhagavad Dharma."

---

> *Vedapáthii bhavet viprah,*
> *brahma jánáti bráhmanah.*
>
> ("One who reads the Vedas may be a *vipra*, but only one who knows Brahman is a Brahmin.")

# Direct Proof and Revelation of Facts

In the course of His lifetime, Baba gave several indisputably authentic and explicitly clear demonstrations. There is a natural tendency among human beings to be overawed by miracles. Taking advantage of this mentality, ordinary gurus generally indulge in displaying some miracles to gain a larger following. This has become an accepted norm, a practice which only brings personal gain. Baba however never performed any demonstrations in order to impress, or for the sake of displaying miraculous powers. As a great spiritualist, His main purpose was to guide His children on the right path. Through His irrefutable and explicit demonstrations He sought to give His followers the right direction in the pursuit of their spiritual goals.

The practical and convincing demonstrations that Baba gave can be divided into approximately three phases: the Jamalpur phase, the Ranchi phase and the Kolkata phase. During the Jamalpur phase, Baba Himself performed the demonstrations or used Dasharath Dada as His medium. These demonstrations usually threw light on some aspects of life and living. Most often they would be related to incidents connected with a *sadhaka’s* previous lifetime. On such occasions, Baba would invariably call upon Dasharathji. He would spiritually empower him and instruct him to glance into the past life of a particular *sadhaka*. Occasionally Baba would have him describe the mental waves, the colour and emotional state of that particular person. The mental waves of a *dharmic* person would be bright and radiantly white, whereas those of a sinful person would be impure, foul and black in colour. The demonstrations during the Jamalpur phase largely laid bare such aspects of the human mind.

Later, when Baba went on to live in Ranchi, He began to give very different types of demonstrations. Here, Dasharath Dada was no longer His only medium. He would perform the demonstrations through the medium of any other Margii as well. In those days, Baba sought to demonstrate and manifest certain psychic processes and techniques. He also demonstrated various types of *samadhis*. The largest number of demonstrations were performed during this phase. In fact all of 1969 was declared as the "Demonstration Year."

The third phase began when Baba moved to Kolkata and settled down there. During this period He gave demonstrations related to smell *tanmatras* as well as other inferences. Demonstrations on microvita too were performed in this phase.

These then are the three main phases into which Baba's demonstrations can be categorized. The following incident is related to the Jamalpur period.

One day Baba was taking His field walk. He was accompanied by a few devotees and Dasharath Dada was also amongst them. Baba would often empower Dasharathji with supernatural powers. On such occasions Dasharathji would be able to see clearly into the past and the future of a particular person. Gradually he had imbibed much spiritual strength and capability—so much so that even without Baba's specific instructions, he could by his own efforts decipher the past and the future of any individual. But throughout his life, Dasharathji maintained complete control over himself. He was fully aware that any such display of spiritual powers would hurl him into a bottomless pit. He never misused his special powers of omniscience and employed them only under Baba's specific orders.

It was already nightfall and Baba was still taking His field walk. Darkness was creeping over the desolate place. Suddenly Baba exclaimed, "Dasharath! Just look and tell me if you are able to see a disembodied soul?"

"Yes Baba!" Dasharathji promptly replied. Baba continued further: "Take a peep into the immediate past life of this soul and see who he was or what was he then."

Dasharathji replied: "Baba, he was a businessman from Kiul and his name was Ramnaresh Tripathi."

Baba replied: "Quite right! Now look into his future life and tell us into whose womb will he be reborn."

Dasharathji replied: "Baba, his next birth will be in the body of a black dog."

Hearing this Baba said with disgust: "Tsk tsk. How shameful indeed. To think this man came from a respectable and wealthy family. Moreover, he had the rare and golden opportunity of a human birth. But now he has lost it all and will be entering into the body of a dog.

What a pity! He wasted away his human life. All through his life he was devoured by greed. Night and day all he could think of was money. His only aim in life was to acquire wealth, by whatever means possible. No doubt he became very rich; in the end was he able to enjoy that wealth? The ones for whom he earned all this wealth, his heirs, do they even think of him now?"

Baba continued, "Let us suppose that by the will of God, this dog was born again in Kiul itself. Let us suppose also that by God's will, this dog is able to retain the memories of his past life. In this case, due to hunger he would certainly go back to his previous home in order to eat something. After all, he would think that this was his own home, his own wealth and fortune. But how will his own family members, sons, daughters, wife, daughter-in-law react when they see him entering the house? They will shout 'a dog is here!' and will drive him away with a stick. Thereupon the dog will cry and bark and will flee from the house. If at that very moment the Lord were to grace him with the capacity to reason and think, then what would be his thoughts? He will then recall the sins he committed in order to amass this wealth for the sake of his family. He will also recall the wrongs he committed against so many people, on account of which he has had to take rebirth as a dog. He will realize his folly, for the wealth he had so amassed was of no use to him now. Today he cannot get even two meals a day. Surely he wasted away his entire life. Dasharath, just think! What can one gain from such a life or such wealth? Now how many years will he have to spend in animal life before he can once more get a human birth? Likewise today, most people are squandering away their precious human life."

Baba went on to say, "It is immensely difficult to gain a human birth. One acquires it only through divine grace. What a great achievement it is to get a human life. But out of ignorance we ruin this precious life through our own misdeeds. Then one is caught in an endless cycle of births in lower and increasingly more degraded forms of life, till one reaches the lowest state. After countless such births one once again attains this rare and priceless human birth. That is why I say over and over again, every human being must consider his life as precious. With this in mind one should make full and proper utilization of one's life. Every moment should be spent in ideation of the Lord and in performing good deeds. One must fulfill all one's worldly duties and responsibilities and at the same time one must be very clear about one's ultimate goal in life. Remember Him always and surrender all your efforts, whether physical or mental, at His Feet. This alone is the right and ideal way of living."

This is how through demonstrations and 'miracles' Baba continued to guide His disciples and led them to the realization of Truth.

# Neo-Humanism

As the human mind rises above narrowness, it expands to fill with generosity and open-heartedness. The sphere of 'mine-ness' expands moment by moment, and the sense of distance from other beings gradually comes to an end. In the ancient past there was a time when human responsibility only extended to one's clan or group. They did not have the vision to look beyond. My clan, my group, others outside my clan are my enemies – that was their vision. Every clan had its leader. The sphere of 'mine-ness' was limited, the sphere of the 'other' expansive. Later on, the more expansive sense of 'one race' emerged, as did the sense of 'one language'. After the First World War the idea of multinationalism and internationalism grew, and spread further after the Second World War.

After the spread of internationalism, the sense of belonging to one human race grew. Human beings began to understand why there are differences between one human being and another, why the sense of 'otherness' exists. That special school of thought, humanism, which was born out of the European Renaissance of the fifteenth and sixteenth centuries, on reaching the twentieth century became a worldview that embraced the welfare of all peoples. Human beings began to use their intellects, their knowledge, the advances of science and its applications, for the benefit of the entire human race. They began to use every one of the conveniences of nature's gifts for the upliftment of the entire human race, even at the cost of causing harm to animals and birds. It did not matter in the least, whether the greenery of plants and shrubs, whether the forests were completely destroyed. The balance of the universe was being disturbed, the environment was being polluted.

At such a juncture in time, under such circumstances, did Yogesvara Shrii Shrii Anandamurtiji manifest Himself. While He also gave the slogan for human beings to be free and equal, bound to each other by ties of fraternity, He brought a new message with him for humanity. This was 'Neo-Humanism'. Humanism had been human-centric. Human sympathy could only extend to the human race. Baba's 'Neo-Humanism' believes that the smallest of the small, the tiniest living being has a great significance. In Baba's words, "It's not right that the love of the developed human mind be confined to human beings." The entire living and non-living world comes within the sphere of Baba's Neo-Humanism.

Every object has two kinds of values: one is its utility value and the other its existential value. Human beings have up until now accepted the utility value of animals, birds and plants, but not their existential value. Baba emphasized their existential value. An ant is as important to the balance of the universe as a blade of grass. Just as the human mind has a part of the Supreme Consciousness within it, so also does a plant. Just as, even if only for selfish purposes, it is prohibited to kill another human, so also for selfish reasons alone it is prohibited to exploit or destroy animal and plant life. The plant world is just as worthy of love as men and women are.

It is Shrii Shrii Anandamurtiji's extraordinary speciality that He does not merely preach. By putting into practice all that He taught, He has always been an exemplary role-model for His disciples. Around His residence in Kolkata He created a small garden, and He loved each plant as much as He loved the *sadhakas*. He was just as anxious, or perhaps even more, about their care and development as He was of the various helpers in His home. Human beings can use their power of speech to express their needs; plants cannot. This is why they are worthier of greater care and looking after. He had given certain wholetimers special instructions on how to look after the plants. He took especial care of those plants which were not too healthy. He would make arrangements for their treatment and cure, and would ask after them on a daily basis. God forbid, if some plant became sick and dying due to neglect, He would get extremely upset with the worker in charge.

At least once a day Baba would take a round of His garden and Himself look after His plants, giving regular instructions to the helpers. Those workers who were attached to Baba's garden had many a time observed plants blossom when they came close to Baba. It seemed as though they were saying something to Baba. In 1980 Baba started His garden tours for the sake of *sadhakas*. Twelve to sixteen people were selected for each tour, to take a round of the garden with Baba. There would be householders as well as whole-timers included on these tours. Baba's knowledge of plants was immeasurable. On the tour, Baba

would daily elucidate new facts about plant-life. Listeners would never tire, would always be dumbstruck.

In His garden were many plants rare and difficult to obtain. Learning about them would be a refreshing experience for the guests. How Baba planted these rare plants in His garden is a story that is fascinating in itself, and worth listening to. Baba had full knowledge both on the mundane and supramundane plane as to which plant, fruit and flower was found where. He knew well which plants of which region were the best variety. He wished to keep a sample of all the best kinds of fruits, flowers and shrubs in His garden. All workers posted around the world were given this instruction that they should bring the best plants of their sector for His garden. He did not appreciate any other gift. When a worker would bring a special plant for Him, He would be extremely pleased. Listen to what Acarya Dhishuddhananda Avadhuta has to say in this incident:

This incident is of 1981. Those days Baba would send for various kinds of plants from all over the world, and He would then transplant them into His own garden. On the terrace of His home He had a temperature-regulating greenhouse built for plants which loved a cold climate. He would look after newly transplanted plants Himself and would direct His helpers accordingly. Sometimes He would take an hourly report of certain delicate, sensitive plants which had been newly transplanted. As long as its newly planted life did not stabilize He would remain abnormally anxious. Sometimes even important organizational agenda would have to wait. For hours He would talk about this newly acquired plant.

The Dadas would compete with each other to please Baba. Some Dada would bring a rare plant and please Baba, another would bring a foreign plant looking after it with utmost care, over thousands and thousands of miles as a gift for Baba. The greater the dedication, the more Baba would be pleased. Choosing the right plant would often become the specific worry of many Dadas. They would not know what to carry back, such that Baba would be pleased and the plant would be talked about by the workers for some time after.

Often Baba Himself would suggest that in such and such a place, such and such a plant is the best in the world. Sometimes He would directly ask for a certain plant from a certain sector. I had many such experiences. Let me tell you all one by one, so that Harnrahi's pen may make these reminiscences blossom in the delightful groves of devotion.

Those days my posting was in the north. Baba had told me about a rare plant called "silver oak" in English. Baba said, "This tree grows in your area. By any means, bring this plant to me from any nursery." How can a person who is totally illiterate in matters of botany, who has no knowledge of plants whatsoever, search for this rare species in this vast area? Even though I didn't lack in devotion, I was not a Hanuman who had the power to fly and bring the mountain of life-giving herbs to his Rama. Seeing me flush and then turn pale, Baba Himself began to give me directions. He told me that if I went past Manali via the banks of the River Vyasa going towards the mountains, exactly 32 kilometers away you will find a government nursery. If you make inquiries at that nursery you will find the silver oak. When I got to know the exact information and direction of my destination, I heaved a sigh of relief. Curbing my pulsating heart, I left for my destination.

Like the eyes of a bird, my singular goal was that 'silver oak' in the nursery of Himachal Pradesh. Baba had given me such detailed descriptions of the important halt on the way that even if I wanted to I could not miss my goal. Baba had even said that there is a botanical research center in Manali. "A few people of the center will be preparing to leave for the nursery. Arrive in time, so you can accompany them." They will help you in every way.

As per Baba's instructions I took the first train to Candigarh. I then went to Bilaspur by bus. There was a Marga school in Bilaspur. Having completed some organizational duties there, I went on to Kullu, and thence to Manali. In Manali I immediately went to the botanical research center. On making inquiries I found out that two professors were leaving early next morning for the nursery. Having got the right information I halted there in a guest house for the night. The next day afternoon, two professors arrived. When they got to know of my interesting inquiry they were very pleased and offered to take me with them in their jeep.

The jeep mostly passed through areas that were largely defence territory. If I hadn't accompanied those two in the government jeep I would never have been able to get permission to reach my destination. Thinking about the possibility of the miraculous success with which I'd be able to take back from this desolate place a plant for Baba, my

entire body felt vibrated. After travelling 28 kilometers by jeep here was a sudden obstacle on our path. Having reached there, my governmental friends told me, "Swamiji, there is no motorable road to the place. We will have to walk three to four kilometers." My enthusiasm was so great that there was no question of refusing them. I quickly got down, ready to walk the rest of the way.

There was no shortage of mental readiness, but I had not the least experience of walking on mountainous terrain. Thinking of Baba, I started on a difficult expedition. On the banks of the river we three walked ahead. But as we advanced we came upon such an obstacle that completely left me flummoxed. All my enthusiasm melted into nothingness. Between two hills, a tree log lay, serving as a makeshift bridge. Down below was a deep, deep chasm. Those two crawled across on their stomachs as deftly as reptiles. They were very experienced with such crossings. My heart was in my mouth. My tongue and gums became dry as bone. I could not say no, nor could I say yes.

Both of them tried hard to give me courage, but my courage had completely vanished. Finally, taking my *guru* mantra with all my heart, surrendering myself completely to Baba, closing my eyes I cut across. Having somehow made it across I didn't even have the guts to look behind me. Having gone a little further, the exquisite view that I saw is hard to put into words. It's difficult to imagine that nature could create something so enchantingly beautiful. Even a great artist would not have been able to transfer it with his brush on canvas. Even the greatest poet would not have been able to describe that extraordinarily exquisite natural beauty. I was so transfixed by what I saw, that I requested my friends, "I would like to meditate here. Would you please bring me from the nursery the plant that my master has asked for?"

They completely refused to do so, saying, "How can this be? The guru is yours. You have received his orders. You should do this task yourself." Their argument was hard to refute. I had to accept it. But even so I again humbly requested them to allow me to sit there for at least ten minutes in *sadhana*. It was impossible for me to endure the temptation of sitting in *sadhana* in that silent enchanting environment. They accepted my request. I asked for ten minutes, but was engrossed in *sadhana* for thirty minutes. I do not know in which world I lost myself. On opening my eyes I was pleased to see that they had been waiting for me. Extricating myself from my absorbed state of mind I walked on with them.

Walking on for another one and a half kilometers from there I saw all around me nothing but snow. Far into the distance, there was not a sign of greenery. In consternation I asked my fellow-travellers, where would I find this plant here? They consoled me, saying that we would actually find it. I was now beginning to doubt the success of my expedition. But what else was I to do? Holding on to the hope they gave me, I continued to walk ahead. Oh what good luck! Walking a little further I began to see a nursery in the distance. When I told the nursery-in-charge the purpose of my visit, he immediately started digging in the snow, unearthing some plants. Finally he brought me one plant and said, "Here, Swamiji! This is your silver oak."

I felt as though the much-spoken of gods of the Puranas were pouring a rain of flowers down on me from the heavens. With half-closed eyes I rejoiced as I accepted the plant. My heart was happy, but in one little corner of my heart the demon of doubt raised its head. I said, "What you say is right, but how should I believe that this plant is the silver oak?" At heart, I began to fear Baba's displeasure if I took back the wrong plant, apart from the beating that I would get. But he convinced me by saying, "No! This is the real silver oak. You should not worry at all." Satisfied with his assurances I took about 20 to 22 plants with me, so that if a couple died on the way, at least a few would survive.

I returned to Shimla with the plants. There is a Marga school there too. I met there Dada Asiimananandaji, the expert on farming and gardening. He congratulated me on my successful expedition and encouraged me greatly. On reaching there, despite the cold of Shimla, the plants started to wither slightly. It made me wonder how these snow-loving plants would bear the heat of Candigarh. It seemed as though the snow of failure was falling over the blossomed flowers of my heart's desires. It would be more appropriate to say that 'heat began to fall'.

On Asimanandaji's suggestion I made an ice-box, and put those cold-loving plants to rest in there, so that the fierce hot winds of Candigarh would not touch them. In this manner I was successful in bringing live silver oaks to Kolkata. I had heard from other Dadas about Baba's omniscience, but this was the first time I saw proof of it

in my own life. How He directed me to the nursery, how He arranged for me to meet with the government officers – I was a personal witness to these mysteries. My awe-struck joy knew no bounds. I cannot describe the unearthly joy I felt, after I offered Baba's desired *prasad* back to His feet.

### Dates of the Oasis

At the beginning of this chapter it has been mentioned that through Baba the modern principle 'Neo-Humanism' was established for the welfare of humanity. He established the existential value of animal, bird and plant life. In truth Baba established a new spiritual humanism. In this context, a passage from His book *Namah Shivaya Shantaya* (1995, 3rd ed., p. 197) is quoted below:

> At one time people were told that this world is for human enjoyment only, so the existence of all the plants, of all the birds and animals in the world is intended merely to provide objects of enjoyment for human beings. Their hopes and aspirations, their intense desire to live, their pains and pleasures, their affectionate family or community lives are simply without value. However much a baby goat may wish to live, the main consideration is how much meat that kid carries on its frame. This defective philosophy has made people ruthlessly violent – even more dangerous than blood-thirsty tigers. Tigers kill only to fill their stomachs, to preserve their physical existence; whereas human beings kill animals mostly out of greed. Generally people have resorted to hypocrisy to camouflage this instinct of greed; they have killed animals on the pretext of pleasing the gods, while actually their main motive in doing so was to please their own tongues. These are all the inevitable results of such defective philosophies.

In this universe a small mosquito's life has as much importance as human life. A new blade of grass is as significant as a new-born baby. Earlier on we saw how exceptional was His love for botany and how deep was His understanding of plants growing around the world. In this part we will savour the other experiences of this very Avadhuta Dada. Hear them in his own words:

I was completely overwhelmed with Baba's omniscience and His special knowledge of botany, as well as my own personal experience with the silver oak. Later on, Baba again gave me many experiences through which I continued to be wonder-struck. Those days my posting was in Kahira sector. Once during a reporting session, Baba asked me, "Which place in the whole world is famous for the sweetest and most delicious dates?" I have already said that my knowledge of this subject is less than zero. It was best for me to remain silent. Baba exclaimed, "This donkey doesn't know anything about his sector." In front of all Baba put the stamp of an 'ass' on me. Baba then said, "What are the virtues of your sector? You should know that much at least." I continued to remain silent. There was no other way out. After remaining quiet for a while, Baba said, "The dates of Bahrain in the Middle East are the most delicious and of the best quality. The speciality of those dates are that unlike other varieties of date, these are round in shape. They are very fleshy, and have practically no stone."

Baba explained that in the desert wherever there was a fertile oasis, it was possible to find date-palms there. In the endless sea of sand, these oases are like flourishing islands. The best variety of dates is available in the oasis nearest to Bahrain. Baba talked at length about a special oasis 12 kilometers from the city of Bahrain, where one could find the best variety of dates. The information that Baba gave entered into my head like a nail that had been hammered into place.

When I visited Bahrain for the first time, I made inquiries about this particular variety of date. By then I had initiated a few local Muslim brothers. I first asked them about this. One of them was an officer in the Nova Scotia bank. When I introduced him to what Baba had told me, he prepared himself to join me in my search. His readiness gave me courage. We both girded our loins and left to tour the desert. As long as there was a concrete road, we continued down it in our car. As soon as the road ended, the car was of no use. All around us were only sand-dunes, a boundless ocean of sand. With Baba's grace we walked on, shading our heads from the scorching heat with umbrellas.

My fellow Margii brother was slightly hesitant, but my unswerving determination and untiring faith soon possessed him. There was not the slightest hint of moisture in the air. The perspiration that oozed out of our tired bodies would evaporate into thin air like droplets of water on a hot griddle. Having walked a short distance, an elderly Arab gentlemen covered from head to foot in a white garment came close

to us. He asked, "Where do you wish to go?" We told him about the reason for starting out on this journey. He was very pleased and said that we had come to the right place. "One and a half kilometers from here is a beautiful oasis. The dates of that place are extremely sweet."

Hearing how close we were to our destination, we picked up courage and speed. We had barely taken a couple of steps, when our guide called out and stopped us. He called one of his friends and asked him to accompany us, saying that it is very easy to lose one's way in this desert. I felt as though God Himself was helping me at every step. With our new companion we quickly reached our destination. I saw an enchanting oasis covered with a hundred to two hundred date palms, heavily laden with reams of dates. My eyes could not believe the greenery of this oasis in the middle of the land of death. But reality assured me that my doubts had no basis whatsoever.

From this dense desert grove, I carried back a load of ripe, unripe and semi-ripe dates. Bringing them to India, I placed them at Baba's feet. At that memorable moment, my joy and enthusiasm was like that of the king of devotees, Shabari, in the *Ramayana*, when he presented Shrii Rama with ber fruit.

### Cotton and Alphonso Mangoes

On a similar note, Baba once asked me, "Where in the world does one find the best variety of cotton?" Whenever Baba would ask me things related to botany, I would always keep silent, making no qualms about exhibiting my ignorance. I would also understand that there was some significance to this kind of question, and would prepare myself for another expedition, whether a small journey or a long one. When I was silent, Baba also became silent. After a while, he repeated the same question. But I had sworn that before Baba I would never exhibit my knowledge. I knew that my meagre knowledge would hold no quarter before His infinite knowledge. Hence I had faith only in keeping silent.

Once I received Baba's instructions, I merely kept faith in completing the task ahead, and pleasing Him by some means. That is why I never got wrapped into these futile questions and answers. Because of my stubborn silence, Baba became serious. He said, "Transfer this donkey, immediately. He doesn't even know about the countries within his sector. Without ordinary knowledge how must he be doing any work?" In spite of this, I remained silent. Neither did I fear transfer, nor was I attached to my post. My moral strength was nourished by my control over my propensities of fear and attachment. My lack of ego and sense of surrender made me prepared for any eventuality.

As I expected, in a moment Baba's angry mood lapsed into normality, and then He took on His Kalyanasundaram form – His form of supreme benevolence. I had witnessed Baba play out His *liilas* many times before. This time, Baba smiled, and addressing the General Secretary said, "Tell this donkey that the fields near the Aswan Dam on the River Nile is best for the farming of cotton. The cotton produced in that area is the best in the world. It has the longest fibre." I understood this as Baba's suggestion to bring a cotton-plant for Him. On my next trip to Kahira I first completed this task and was able to greatly please Baba.

The last story about the alphonso mangoes is also very interesting. In those days I was posted in Mumbai. This time Baba asked me, "Which is the best mango in the world, and where does it grow?" I had heard that the Alphonso mango of Ratnagiri was the best variety of mango. I was tired of being called an ass before a crowded room, and my certain knowledge of the alphonso mango was urging me to speak. I said without hesitating, "The alphonso mango of Ratnagiri district in Maharashtra." Then Baba addressed all present, "Oh look! This boy knows so much! He's so intelligent! So bright!" Hearing this praise in front of all, I felt most proud. My ego was well-satisfied. How was I to know that Baba was praising me to the skies only to shatter my pride? I arrived at this understanding only after being badly hurt. At that moment, however, Baba, pleased, patted me on the back and cheerfully instructed me to bring Him some alphonso mangoes.
